# File Transfer Pipeline TODO

This is the separate internal TODO tracker for the DinoX file-transfer pipeline work. `docs/internal/DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md` remains the roadmap-facing reconciliation table; this file records the implementation slices so the pipeline work does not restart from scratch or duplicate completed steps.

## Scope Rules

- File-transfer scenarios are tracked separately: unencrypted, OMEMO, XEP-0027 legacy OpenPGP, and XEP-0373/XEP-0374 OpenPGP for XMPP.
- Audio/video calls are out of scope because they use the RTP/WebRTC/Jingle media stack.
- Audio and video messages are in scope because they are file send/receive flows.
- OpenPGP is part of the pipeline. The current OpenPGP integration is functional and ready for pipeline work, but it is not the sole foundation and it does not prove the full pipeline is complete.
- Heavy transfer work must move behind worker/job boundaries so UI and chat interaction stay responsive.

## Internal Priority Definitions

- **P1a**: Regression in non-sensitive file-transfer control plane behavior (e.g. selection logic, metadata, or local persistence) that is user-visible but does not impact transfer completion integrity.
- **P1b**: Regression in high-risk transfer control plane edge-cases that can cause failed/cancelled transfer states, crash-adjacent behavior, or stuck transfer lifetime (for example outgoing Jingle cancellation/error cleanup paths).
- **P0**: Confirmed crash or data-loss regression that affects application stability or corrupts persisted transfer state.

## Integration Gates / Matrix Proof

Purpose: prove that the accumulated pipeline slices work together before more feature implementation continues. Focused tests stay useful, but this gate tracks what has been proven across the full transfer model and what is still only proven in isolated slices.

This matrix is risk-based, not a flat list of equally important combinations. The first proof target is the highest-risk path for each security scenario, then conversation/platform/UI coverage expands from there. Do not treat `GROUPCHAT_PM` as the same problem as private members-only MUC file transfer: `GROUPCHAT_PM` is a separate MUC private-message conversation type, while the private-MUC safety gate is about a members-only/non-anonymous groupchat with verified recipient certainty. OMEMO currently rejects `GROUPCHAT_PM`; keep that as a negative gate unless the full recipient model is redesigned.

### Matrix Dimensions

| Dimension | Required coverage |
|-----------|-------------------|
| Security scenario / protocol variant | Unencrypted; OMEMO with OMEMO1 and OMEMO2 tracked separately where supported; XEP-0027 legacy OpenPGP; XEP-0373/XEP-0374 OpenPGP for XMPP. |
| Transport/source | HTTP upload/body URL, SFS/ESFS where applicable, Jingle file-transfer classification where applicable. |
| Conversation type | 1:1, public MUC, private MUC, and MUC private messages (`GROUPCHAT_PM`) as a separate recipient model. OMEMO1/OMEMO2 known-member private-MUC success is locally proven, OpenPGP verified private-MUC `GROUPCHAT` and MUC-PM local proofs now exist, and public MUC still requires separate recipient modelling before enablement. |
| Outcome | Success, user cancellation, provider/metadata failure, decrypt/tamper/signature failure, hash mismatch, final commit collision. |
| UI surface | Generic file item, image preview, audio message, video message, preview/open/save/export. |
| Platform | Linux and Windows. Windows must use the dist build path for user-runtime checks when OpenPGP/GPG behavior is involved. |
| Size policy | Small file smoke, 10 MB, 50 MB, 200 MB. Large-file proof is blocked on durable job/retry/storage ownership. |

### Completion Dashboard

As of 2026-05-31 the pipeline is in the matrix-proof phase, not the architecture phase. Treat this dashboard as the short closure view; the detailed evidence stays in the completed-slices table and run log below.

| Area | State | Closure meaning |
|------|-------|-----------------|
| Worker, verify-before-release, send metadata and receive cleanup contracts | CLOSED for focused local proof | Existing focused suites cover the worker lanes, publish lane, no-plaintext-before-verify, send metadata, HTTP slot/PUT/cancel and decrypt/tamper/signature failure boundaries. Re-run the focused baseline when pipeline files change. |
| Linux/headless and Windows-MSYS2 headless HTTP/private-MUC runtime | CLOSED for recorded DinoX-to-DinoX scenarios | Unencrypted, OMEMO1, controlled OMEMO2/SCE, XEP-0374 and XEP-0027 private-MUC HTTP smokes are recorded. This does not replace GUI or durable-job proof. |
| Jingle/S5B runtime and privacy routing | CLOSED for recorded direct, proxy, Tor, temporary public-candidate, stable public proxy/onion and raw-direct proofs | Independent stdlib sender, DinoX pair, proxy no-direct/proxy-success, real-Tor no-direct, onion, temporary public-candidate, stable public XEP-0065 proxy, packaged GUI Tor-onion receiver, headless stable raw-direct server-side and packaged GUI stable raw-direct receiver/open/export slices are recorded. The 2026-06-01 raw-direct closure uses the self-owned public server `mail.dinox.im` / `45.84.199.125:47777`, not a temporary tunnel or LAN NAT mapping: UFW owns the port, the server-side stdlib sender binds the advertised port and the receiver reaches it through isolated Tor account-proxy with Tor `SENTCONNECT`, S5B listener connection, `candidate-used` and receive-store completion. The 2026-06-02 packaged GUI raw-direct closure proves the same public endpoint through real `dist\dinox.exe`, default-open and native Save-as export. Encrypted Tor-routed GUI Jingle variants stay separate from the unencrypted receiver proofs. |
| Encrypted Jingle/JET | CLOSED for local, Linux/headless, Windows-MSYS2 headless, Windows `dist` runtime, Windows packaged GUI DinoX sender OMEMO1/OMEMO2 and normal DinoX-to-DinoX packaged GUI OMEMO1 receiver/open/export; PARTIAL for product v2-only receiver mode and transport variants | OMEMO1/JET and OMEMO2/JET headless plus packaged `dist` DLL/plugin runtime are recorded. Packaged Windows GUI DinoX sender selection is closed for OMEMO1/JET and OMEMO2/JET Direct/Jingle-S5B, and packaged Windows GUI OMEMO1/JET receiver/open/export is recorded for normal, stable public proxy and stable public raw-direct transports. A normal DinoX GUI receiver is mixed OMEMO1/OMEMO2, so the product-path receiver/open/export proof for DinoX-to-DinoX is OMEMO1/JET; a product v2-only OMEMO2/JET receiver mode and encrypted Tor-onion variants remain separate optional gates. |
| Windows packaged GUI user path | PARTIAL | Unencrypted HTTP/SFS, unencrypted Jingle/S5B, OMEMO1/JET Jingle/S5B and OMEMO2/JET Jingle/S5B sender selection are recorded with real `dist\dinox.exe`; unencrypted direct-candidate, unencrypted stable public proxy, unencrypted stable public raw-direct, unencrypted Tor-onion, OMEMO1/JET Jingle/S5B, OMEMO1/JET stable public proxy, OMEMO1/JET stable public raw-direct and OpenPGP HTTP/SFS packaged GUI receive-store/default-open/save-as export are now recorded too. Remaining closure work for file exchange is encrypted Tor-onion transport variants if they stay in scope. |

### Minimal Risk-Based Matrix

| Scenario | 1:1 | Public MUC | Private members-only MUC | Platform proof |
|----------|-----|------------|--------------------------|----------------|
| Unencrypted | Success, cancel, provider failure, hash mismatch, commit collision. | Success, cancel, provider failure, hash mismatch, commit collision. | Same as public MUC, because no encryption-recipient proof is required. | Linux Gate 5; Windows before pipeline complete. |
| OMEMO1 | Success, cancel, missing secret, ESFS/AES-GCM tamper. | Encrypted public-MUC send must remain blocked or negative-tested. | Local known-member success proof exists; cancel, tamper, real server affiliation/member-list interop and Windows runtime remain required. | Linux focused proof first; Windows runtime before pipeline complete. |
| OMEMO2 where supported | Do not promote SFS/ESFS send until device capability and SCE wrapping are proven. | Block/negative-test until group recipient capability is proven. | Block until recipient certainty and all-device OMEMO2 support are proven. | Linux proof before enablement; Windows before complete. |
| XEP-0027 OpenPGP | Success, cancel, unsigned, wrong signer, no trusted key. | Only with complete member-key coverage; otherwise block/negative-test. | Same as public MUC, plus no self-only send when member list is unknown. | Linux focused proof first; Windows GPG runtime before complete. |
| XEP-0373/XEP-0374 OpenPGP | Success, cancel, wrong signer, no trusted key, key-refresh state. | Only with complete member-key coverage; otherwise block/negative-test. | Same as public MUC, plus PubSub key lifecycle and no self-only send when member list is unknown. | Linux focused proof first; Windows GPG runtime before complete. |

### Always-On Invariants

- No plaintext path, metadata, size, preview phase or `COMPLETE` state may be published before verification/authentication succeeds.
- Cancellation or failure must not publish a transfer and must remove or retain temporary files only according to explicit lease ownership.
- The UI observes phase/progress/error/final results and must not perform widget-local decrypt or preview bypasses.
- Long crypto/hash/store work must run behind the transfer worker/job boundary; short final publish/cleanup work must stay off the default long-work lane.
- OMEMO1, OMEMO2, XEP-0027 OpenPGP, XEP-0373/XEP-0374 OpenPGP and unencrypted flows remain separate proof boundaries.
- Encrypted sends must not leak cleartext OOB/SFS/ESFS metadata outside the encrypted message body unless a future per-message encryption-phase model proves it safe.
- Audio/video calls remain out of scope; audio/video messages remain in scope as file-transfer flows.

| Gate | Scope | Current state | Pass requirement before continuing |
|------|-------|---------------|------------------------------------|

Any failure becomes a blocking fix before new pipeline feature work. |

| Gate 3: Send wire/metadata proof | Verify that send paths preserve body-only encrypted metadata, HTTP sender requirement, slot/upload/announce phases, worker-backed hashing and outgoing local-save cleanup. | PARTIAL | No encrypted send publishes unsafe outer metadata. FileManager send orchestration now proves external encrypt-before-slot, final metadata handoff, and public `send_file()` local-save-before-transport-failure propagation; remaining real-provider/runtime send gaps stay listed before OMEMO2 SFS/ESFS promotion. |

Real server affiliation/member-list interop, public MUC, Windows runtime and foreign-client delivery remain open. |
| Gate 6: Large-file and resume proof | Bounded memory, durable job ownership, retry/resume and 10 MB/50 MB/200 MB runtime tests. | TODO | Must not be marked complete until durable jobs and restart/retry ownership exist. |

- `383ff93e` adds a synthetic XEP-0374/OpenPGP body-only send proof through public `FileManager.send_file()`, the real HTTP sender and local HTTPS `PUT`: external PGP file encryption happens before slot request, the upload is `.pgp`/`application/pgp-encrypted`, the encoded `<openpgp/>` payload contains the body URL, and the outer stanza/local sources do not leak cleartext SFS/OOB/attachment URL metadata.
- `10d22dc2` adds the matching synthetic XEP-0027/OpenPGP legacy body-only send proof through public `FileManager.send_file()`, the real HTTP sender and local HTTPS `PUT`: external PGP file encryption happens before slot request, the upload is `.pgp`/`application/pgp-encrypted`, the encoded `<x xmlns='jabber:x:encrypted'/>` payload contains the body URL, and the outer stanza/local sources do not leak cleartext SFS/OOB/attachment URL metadata.
- `65f66ee3` adds the first real GPG file-encryption helper proof for OpenPGP file sends: an optional isolated live-keyring test runs `GPGHelper.encrypt_file_to_path()` and `decrypt_file_to_path()` with generated test keys, strict full primary fingerprints, real GPG `--sign --encrypt`, preserved plaintext filename and verified signer fingerprint. This proves the GPG file crypto boundary, not the full FileManager/HTTP/OpenPGP-manager runtime send path.
- `d7ed3462` moves that proof into the real legacy `PgpFileEncryptor` path: the optional live test now drives `PgpFileEncryptor.encrypt_file()` through a real OpenPGP `Manager`, Dino/OpenPGP DB account-contact key state and real GPG, proving selected XEP-0027 protocol, encrypted metadata, signer verification and recipient-key presence in the OpenPGP packet. - `f9bdf144`, `41212c7d`, `1cd733d5` and `67fddaeb` extend the live OpenPGP proof through the XEP-0373/XEP-0374 and legacy XEP-0027 manager/DB/GPG boundaries and into public `FileManager.send_file()` with the real HTTP sender. The current highest OpenPGP send proof reaches pinned local HTTPS `PUT` success for both XEP-0374 and XEP-0027, decrypts captured `.pgp` upload bodies, verifies signer and packet recipients, and proves encrypted body-url stanzas without cleartext outer file metadata leakage.

- `b4b19ce8` adds the first real OMEMO2/SCE file-body-url precondition proof. `Tests for omemo` proves v2-only recipient and own devices use the OMEMO2/SCE namespace with recipient/own-device key groups and recipient decrypt back to the exact `aesgcm://...#ivkey` body URL, while a recipient with any trusted OMEMO1 device falls back to legacy OMEMO1 and does not target the v2-only device as a legacy key recipient. - `a25dbefb` connects the real OMEMO2/SCE precondition boundary into public `FileManager.send_file()` plus the real HTTP sender for the v2-only positive path. `Tests for omemo` now drives production `FileManager.start()`, real `HttpFileSender`, fake XEP-0363 slot response, pinned local HTTPS `Soup.Server`, real libsoup `PUT`, real `Manager.start()` with OMEMO2 encryptor wiring, `MessageProcessor.pre_message_send`, `TrustManager`, OMEMO DB state and libomemo OMEMO2 sessions. - `9f99f415` adds the matching public FileManager/HTTP mixed-device fallback proof. `Tests for omemo` now keeps OMEMO2 infrastructure active while recipient and own secondary devices include both OMEMO1 and OMEMO2 entries, then proves the real public send path falls back to legacy OMEMO1 body-only, does not write an OMEMO2/SCE stanza, does not target v2-only devices as legacy key recipients, decrypts the body URL through the recipient legacy store, and decrypts the captured HTTPS PUT back to the original payload. This closes the focused public mixed v1/v2 fallback send boundary, but not PubSub fetch/publish, private-MUC, Windows or interop proof.
- The current OpenPGP MUC-PM slice proves the local `GROUPCHAT_PM` recipient model for file sends without treating it as public MUC or ordinary private-MUC group delivery. XEP-0374 and XEP-0027 MUC-PM sends use the occupant room/nick as the outer stanza target, the cached real bare JID for recipient key readiness and XEP-0374 signcrypt recipient identity, and a key/protocol/recipient snapshot for body-url announcement after upload. Normal MUC-PM text remains outside that file snapshot path. The receive-side proof now also covers persisted/manual download resolving the full `GROUPCHAT_PM` conversation before the bare room, so OpenPGP signature verification can use the real-JID sender model.
- The current branch does not yet prove the full integrated runtime matrix across all four security scenarios, UI surfaces, Linux/Windows and conversation types.

Current remote scripted-raw Jingle S5B smoke addendum: `/RemoteJingleS5bSmoke/incoming_raw_sender_s5b_env` now proves the same real-server receive-store boundary with a sender stream that bypasses DinoX `offer_file_stream()`, detaches the sender-side Jingle handlers and emits the XEP-0234/XEP-0260 Jingle/S5B IQs plus S5B byte stream through a scripted path. Current remote standalone foreign-sender Jingle S5B smoke addendum: `scripts/foreign-jingle-s5b-sender.py` now provides a stdlib-only XMPP/Jingle/S5B sender for the existing foreign-client receiver harness. It performs STARTTLS, SASL PLAIN, resource bind, raw XEP-0234/XEP-0260 IQ exchange and S5B byte transfer without importing DinoX or `xmpp-vala`, while the receiver remains the real DinoX `JingleFileProvider` plus `FileManager` receive-store path. This closes an independent test-sender runtime boundary on the real server; additional packaged-client behavior beyond the recorded Libervia proof, Windows, encrypted Jingle payload and durable/server-ack gates remain separate.

Historical status addendum at `3f3ab0c6`: this slice added the OpenPGP MUC/private-MUC negative safety gate. It proved `GROUPCHAT` and `GROUPCHAT_PM` PGP file sends failed closed before slot/upload/announcement even if the encryptor was called directly, while preserving one-to-one OpenPGP file sends. Later slices now prove positive verified private-MUC `GROUPCHAT` delivery and the separate local `GROUPCHAT_PM` model for XEP-0374 and XEP-0027; public MUC, remote interop, broader Windows file-transfer runtime and durable/server-ack behavior remain separate work.

Historical status addendum at `4ab096c0`: this slice added only the first OpenPGP private-MUC recipient-candidate model. It rejected private MUC PMs instead of guessing from `room/nick`, rejected public/unverified rooms, rejected missing or self-only member candidates, and accepted cached non-self member candidates only as candidates. Later slices add complete affiliation certainty, key readiness, key material, positive verified private-MUC send enablement and the separate local MUC-PM model; public MUC remains a separate recipient model.

Historical status addendum at `9c8d96e6`: this slice completed the OpenPGP private-MUC affiliation-snapshot certainty boundary for candidates. The resolver requires live private-room features and completed member/admin/owner affiliation queries, uses a separate query snapshot instead of the general affiliation cache, invalidates stale live features/snapshots on failed refresh and local leave, and tests stale offline cache, stale live cache, local leave, incomplete snapshots, re-query replacement, outcast/non-member pollution, presence-cache pollution, self filtering and duplicate recipients. At this gate, per-recipient OpenPGP key readiness and positive delivery were still open; later slices now close key readiness, key material and positive verified private-MUC `GROUPCHAT` delivery.

Historical status addendum at `cf0acadd`: this slice completed the next OpenPGP private-MUC pre-send boundary by proving per-recipient key readiness without enabling delivery. XEP-0373/XEP-0374 candidates need stored XMPP keys and session-fresh state, XEP-0027 candidates need stored legacy keys and live announcements, and accidental PubSub fetches are covered by tests with the key manager and PubSub/OpenPGP modules wired. Later slices now add key material, positive verified private-MUC `GROUPCHAT` delivery and the separate local `GROUPCHAT_PM` model while keeping public MUC separate.

Historical status addendum at `2a28f69a`: this slice completed the next OpenPGP private-MUC pre-send boundary by resolving actual file-encryption key material from the proven candidate and readiness gates. The resolver returns the account key first plus every ready recipient key for the selected OpenPGP mode, uses direct DB state instead of chat/send-discovery helpers, and proves the XEP-0373/XEP-0374 stale-key and XEP-0027 inactive-announcement boundaries without PubSub/key-fetch side effects. Later slices now wire that material into positive verified private-MUC `GROUPCHAT` delivery and add the separate local `GROUPCHAT_PM` model; public MUC remains fail-closed.

Historical status addendum for the OpenPGP private-MUC send-enablement slice: that change wired the proven OpenPGP private-MUC key material into the real FileManager/HTTP send path for verified private-MUC `GROUPCHAT` conversations. At that gate, `PgpFileEncryptor` allowed only verified private-MUC groupchat sends, kept `GROUPCHAT_PM` and public/unverified MUCs fail-closed, validated the selected account key for XEP-0374 `xmpp:` UID or XEP-0027 email UID, and snapshotted key/protocol material so the uploaded `.pgp` payload and encrypted body-url announcement could not diverge if DB key state changed between upload and announcement. Later MUC-PM work now covers the separate local `GROUPCHAT_PM` model. Live tests prove positive XEP-0374 and XEP-0027 private-MUC sends plus an invalid XEP-0374 account-key fail-before-slot case. Remaining OpenPGP group work is public-MUC recipient modelling, remote affiliation/keyserver interop, broader Windows file-transfer runtime and durable/server-ack proof.

Current keyserver addendum: the current keyserver slice proves the one-to-one XEP-0027 missing-key path that was still open after `79e3b95d`. A local HKP `Soup.Server` supplies Bob's public key to an isolated client keyring only after verified signed presence triggers the existing keyserver discovery path. The file-transfer receive path is deliberately not allowed to fetch keys; it only validates a Bob-signed incoming file after the presence/keyserver path has already imported and trusted the legacy key. Remaining keyserver work is external OpenPGP HKP/VKS behavior plus foreign-client delivery, not another local receive-side discovery path and not a broad XMPP-server matrix.

Current affiliation-IQ addendum: the current private-MUC follow-up does not add another crypto mode or delivery format. It tightens the prerequisite proof for private-MUC recipients by showing that DinoX refreshes live room info, queries `member`, `admin` and `owner` via the real XEP-0045 module path to the bare room JID, accepts from-less affiliation results for the requested room, rejects explicit non-exact result `from` JIDs including same-bare full JIDs, and keeps forbidden/error affiliation results from completing the snapshot. OpenPGP candidate resolution and OMEMO FileManager/HTTP upload gating remain fail-closed on incomplete snapshots. The next remaining boundary is real remote-server private-MUC affiliation/member-list smoke with permission variance; this local proof only prevents restarting the earlier candidate/snapshot discussion.

Current OMEMO snapshot addendum: OMEMO private-MUC file sends no longer rely on the broader general affiliation cache as a sufficient recipient source. The send path now needs live private-room features plus the complete member/admin/owner query snapshot before HTTP upload and before OMEMO stanza encryption. The current forbidden-admin/owner proof confirms a partial member-only snapshot stays incomplete and fails before slot, upload or stanza generation. This keeps OMEMO1 and OMEMO2 wire behavior unchanged while aligning OMEMO and OpenPGP on the same private-MUC recipient-certainty boundary.

Current MUC affiliation freshness addendum: a completed member/admin/owner query baseline remains mandatory. After that baseline, validated room-admin results and MUC presence carrying a real bare JID plus member/admin/owner affiliation are authoritative deltas and keep the snapshot current; they cannot complete a partial baseline. Starting a fresh affiliation query revokes cryptographic freshness until that exact generation succeeds, while malformed, failed or superseded results preserve the last display projection without authorizing an encrypted send. Read-only affiliation results without `from` retain the documented request-bound compatibility behavior; any explicit `from` must still match the exact bare room JID, and mutation IQs remain strict.

Current OMEMO self-only addendum: a complete private-MUC snapshot with only the local account is now an explicit fail-closed proof point. This matters because the original pipeline audit required DinoX to distinguish an unknown member list from a verified solo room, and neither case may silently become self-only encrypted file delivery.

Current HTTP cancel addendum: the current unencrypted HTTP slices lift the lower-level cancel-after-request and blocked request-body/backpressure proofs into the public `FileManager.send_file()` path. Pinned local HTTPS `PUT` now covers success, status failure, cancel-after-SFS-share, cancel-after-full-request and cancel-while-request-body-blocked. The FileManager path persists `FAILED/cancelled` without `COMPLETE`, without a source attachment, and without any in-memory or persisted SFS HTTP source for the cancellation cases. This closes the local public unencrypted FileManager HTTP cancellation matrix while leaving encrypted variants, remote server interop, broader Windows file-transfer runtime and durable/server-ack behavior separate. These slices do not touch OMEMO1 body-only, OMEMO2/SCE, OpenPGP body-url semantics or private-MUC recipient gates.

Current OMEMO cancel addendum: the current encrypted cancellation slice starts with public one-to-one OMEMO1 because it reuses the already proven real OMEMO manager/session and public FileManager/HTTP success boundary. The test cancels only after the complete encrypted `PUT` body has reached a pinned local HTTPS server, then proves DinoX stays in `FAILED/cancelled` without entering `ANNOUNCING`, without creating a body-url message id, without assigning a file-sharing id, without emitting OMEMO1/OMEMO2 message stanzas, and without persisting a local HTTP source. This preserves OMEMO1 body-only semantics and does not claim OMEMO2/SCE, OpenPGP, blocked-body encrypted cancellation, remote interop, broader Windows file-transfer runtime or durable/server-ack proof.

Current OMEMO blocked-body addendum: the current follow-up moves the public one-to-one OMEMO1 cancellation proof to the backpressure boundary. The test replaces the upload stream only after `UPLOADING`, sends one controlled encrypted-body chunk, waits until the local HTTPS server observes PUT headers, then cancels while the request body is blocked. It proves no full request body is accepted, no body-url announcement or message stanza is written, no source/link metadata is published and the transfer remains `FAILED/cancelled`. This preserves OMEMO1 body-only semantics and does not claim OMEMO2/SCE, OpenPGP, remote interop, broader Windows file-transfer runtime or durable/server-ack proof.

Current OMEMO2 cancel addendum: the current follow-up repeats the cancel-after-full-PUT boundary with a v2-only OMEMO setup and active OMEMO2 encryptor wiring. Because the transfer is cancelled before `prepare_body_url_announcement()`, the correct result is no SCE stanza, no legacy fallback stanza and no body-url metadata at all. The test proves the v2-only path reaches encrypted HTTP upload, then remains `FAILED/cancelled` before message/link/source publication. This preserves OMEMO2/SCE selection and fallback semantics instead of changing them.

Current OMEMO2 blocked-body addendum: the current follow-up moves the public one-to-one v2-only OMEMO cancellation proof to the backpressure boundary. The test uses the active OMEMO2 encryptor wiring, replaces the upload stream only after `UPLOADING`, sends one controlled encrypted-body chunk, waits until the local HTTPS server observes PUT headers, then cancels while the request body is blocked. It proves no full request body is accepted, no SCE stanza, legacy fallback stanza, body-url announcement, message link or source metadata is written, and the transfer remains `FAILED/cancelled`. This preserves OMEMO2/SCE selection and fallback semantics and leaves OpenPGP blocked-body variants as the next send boundary.

Current OpenPGP XEP-0374 cancel addendum: the current follow-up repeats the cancel-after-full-PUT boundary with a public one-to-one XEP-0374/OpenPGP-for-XMPP setup and the real `PgpFileEncryptor`. Because the transfer is cancelled before `prepare_body_url_announcement()`, the correct result is no XEP-0374 stanza, no XEP-0027 fallback stanza and no body-url metadata at all. The test proves the `.pgp` upload is real GPG output with valid signer and account/contact recipients, then remains `FAILED/cancelled` before message/link/source publication. This preserves OpenPGP body-url semantics instead of changing them.

Current OpenPGP XEP-0374 blocked-body addendum: the current follow-up moves the public one-to-one XEP-0374 cancellation proof to the backpressure boundary. It keeps the real `PgpFileEncryptor` and live-GPG setup, but replaces the upload stream only after the real encrypted `.pgp` size has been computed and the transfer enters `UPLOADING`. The controlled stream sends a real encrypted first chunk, waits for local HTTPS PUT headers, then cancels while the body is blocked. The proof shows no complete request body, no XEP-0374 stanza, no XEP-0027 fallback stanza, no body-url message/link/source metadata and persisted `FAILED/cancelled`. This preserves OpenPGP body-url semantics. The matching legacy XEP-0027 boundary is covered by the current XEP-0027 blocked-body addendum.

Current OpenPGP XEP-0027 cancel addendum: the current follow-up repeats the cancel-after-full-PUT boundary with a public one-to-one legacy XEP-0027 OpenPGP setup and the real `PgpFileEncryptor`. Because the transfer is cancelled before `prepare_body_url_announcement()`, the correct result is no legacy XEP-0027 stanza, no XEP-0374 fallback stanza and no body-url metadata at all. The test proves the `.pgp` upload is real GPG output with valid signer and account/contact recipients, then remains `FAILED/cancelled` before message/link/source publication. This preserves OpenPGP body-url semantics instead of changing them.

Current OpenPGP XEP-0027 blocked-body addendum: the current follow-up closes the symmetric public one-to-one legacy OpenPGP backpressure boundary. It keeps the real `PgpFileEncryptor` and live-GPG setup, replaces the upload stream only after the real encrypted `.pgp` size has been computed and the transfer enters `UPLOADING`, sends a real encrypted first chunk, waits for local HTTPS PUT headers, and then cancels while the body is blocked. The proof shows no complete request body, no legacy XEP-0027 stanza, no XEP-0374 fallback stanza, no body-url message/link/source metadata and persisted `FAILED/cancelled`. This preserves OpenPGP body-url semantics and closes the local public 1:1 blocked-body cancellation set for unencrypted, OMEMO1, OMEMO2, XEP-0374 and XEP-0027 sends.

Current OpenPGP MUC-PM addendum: this slice enables the separate `GROUPCHAT_PM` file-transfer recipient model for OpenPGP without changing public MUC or OMEMO behavior. It requires a full room/nick conversation, cached occupant real JID, live private members-only non-anonymous room features and mode-specific key readiness for the real bare recipient. The send path snapshots key/protocol/recipient material before upload so XEP-0374 and XEP-0027 body-url announcements stay bound to the uploaded `.pgp` payload even if key state changes after `PUT`. The outer stanza remains addressed to room/nick, while XEP-0374 signcrypt uses the real bare JID as the recipient identity. Normal MUC-PM text uses the existing message path and is not blocked by missing file snapshot state. Receive-side OpenPGP verification resolves MUC-PM senders through cached real JID, and `FileManager.download_file()` now resolves persisted/manual downloads to the full `GROUPCHAT_PM` conversation before falling back to the bare room.

Current OpenPGP public-MUC fail-closed addendum: this proof does not enable public MUC file delivery. It hardens the existing `GROUPCHAT` FileManager/HTTP fail-closed boundary by using an explicitly public room-info response, a cached Bob occupant real JID and Bob's cached XEP-0373 key marker. The test proves DinoX still fails before XEP-0363 slot request, upload, body-url message, file-sharing id, persisted HTTP source or `COMPLETE`. Public-MUC recipient modelling remains a separate design and proof gate after remote private-MUC interop and the remaining matrix items.

Current OpenPGP public-MUC error-surfacing addendum: this refinement keeps `PgpFileEncryptor.can_encrypt_file()` as the strict readiness gate, adds an optional FileManager encryptor-rejection reason hook, and surfaces the concrete OpenPGP MUC/private-room requirement only after a sender has been found but no compatible encryptor can claim the transfer. The public-MUC and MUC-PM FileManager tests still prove no XEP-0363 slot request, upload, body-url message, file-sharing id, persisted HTTP source or `COMPLETE`; they now also prove the persisted failure reason is specific instead of the generic "No sender/encryptor combination available". The matching libdino proof covers the generic FileManager selection boundary directly: specific encryptor reasons are used only after an available sender exists, while the no-sender path stays generic and does not query encryptor-specific reasons. This does not enable public MUC delivery and does not change OMEMO1, OMEMO2/SCE or OpenPGP body-url semantics.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 outbound description addendum: this slice narrows the first Jingle proof to the XML description boundary used before a full session or transport offer exists. `offer_file_stream()` now uses a shared `create_file_description()` helper so the production path and tests build the same XEP-0234 `<description/>`. The helper emits `<size>0</size>` for zero-byte files instead of omitting `<size/>`, rejects negative sizes before constructing an offer, preserves basename/media-type fields, and keeps the existing XEP-0300 hash TODO visible by not adding a `<hash/>` claim. This does not change OMEMO1 body-only, OMEMO2/SCE, OpenPGP body-url semantics, HTTP upload behavior, S5B/IBB transport selection, remote interop or durable/server-ack state.

Current Jingle XEP-0260 S5B network-policy addendum: this slice is a local regression proof for the existing proxy/Tor Jingle SOCKS5 policy. The tests construct S5B transport parameters directly and prove that `set_network_policy(false, ...)` suppresses local `DIRECT` candidates, reports the transport unavailable when no discovered S5B proxy exists, and still allows discovered proxy candidates so Jingle S5B is not globally removed. This protects the remembered account SOCKS5/Tor privacy boundary, but it is not a full Jingle session, not a live proxy handshake, not a foreign-client incoming Jingle-S5B smoke and not executed remote interop.

Current Jingle XEP-0260 S5B direct prepared-listener cleanup addendum: this slice proves the local direct listener lifecycle that earlier parser-failure cleanup could only protect by code inspection. The focused `Socks5Audit` test creates production S5B transport parameters with local direct candidates enabled, extracts the published direct candidate and `dstaddr`, completes the same RFC1928/XEP-0260 no-auth SOCKS5 handshake expected by `LocalListener`, then disposes the transport parameters. The proof verifies that disposal closes the accepted connection and refuses a second connection to the same listener port. This remains a local transport-parameter/listener proof; it does not drive Jingle `candidate-used`, expose a `FileTransfer.stream`, transfer file payload over S5B, activate a proxy, exercise Tor/proxy runtime, or prove remote/foreign-client interop.

Current Jingle XEP-0260 S5B incoming candidate-used byte-flow addendum: this slice moves one step past listener lifecycle into local direct-S5B negotiation. The focused `JingleFTAudit` test drives an incoming XEP-0234 offer with XEP-0260 S5B transport through `Jingle.Module.handle_iq_set()`, starts `FileTransfer.accept()` asynchronously, captures DinoX's `session-accept` direct candidate and `dstaddr`, completes the loopback SOCKS5 handshake against the prepared listener, injects peer `transport-info/candidate-used` for that local candidate, then proves `FileTransfer.stream` is fulfilled, the declared payload can be read, stream close sends Jingle `session-terminate` with `success`, and no session state remains. This remains local fake-stream direct-S5B proof only; it does not connect DinoX to a remote candidate, activate a proxy, exercise Tor/proxy runtime, prove foreign-client delivery, enter the libdino receive-store path, prove Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior, or cover Windows/durable/server-ack behavior.

Current Jingle XEP-0391 JET S5B encrypted-payload addendum: this slice closes the first local encrypted Jingle payload boundary without claiming full production runtime readiness. The focused `JingleFTAudit` test registers the real `Xep.Jet.Module` security precondition, attaches a test-only JET envelope and stream cipher, parses a `<security xmlns='urn:xmpp:jingle:jet:0'/>` node from an incoming XEP-0234/XEP-0260 S5B offer, verifies the resulting `FileTransfer.security`, completes the same local direct-S5B candidate-used negotiation, injects ciphertext on the raw peer socket, and proves `FileTransfer.stream` yields the original plaintext before success termination and Jingle cleanup. The production `EncryptedStream` wrapper is now idempotent and best-effort on close so cleanup can close wrapper streams and the underlying transport without fatal duplicate-close warnings. The follow-up local JET-OMEMO AES-GCM slice proves real `Dino.Plugins.JetOmemo.AesGcmCipher` payload+tag success, tampered-tag fail-closed before first plaintext, empty-payload explicit authentication, async-only input compatibility, and declared-size plus 16-byte-tag bounding; `FileTransferInputStream` configures marked authenticated streams with the declared plaintext size and checks authentication before Jingle EOF/success. The local legacy-OMEMO1/JET envelope slice proves real `OmemoEncryptor`/`OmemoDecryptor` operation with seeded trusted OMEMO1 device/session state, real `Jet.Module` create/parse flow, encrypted key envelope without OMEMO message payload, wrong active recipient-device rejection, recovered transport key/IV and parsed-secret payload decryption. The sender-side fail-fast slice makes empty/failed JET-OMEMO envelope creation throw before outgoing `session-initiate`, with cleanup coverage proving no out-stanza, no session/promise state and terminated/disposed content/transport state. The same-rid wrong-sender hardening slice rejects a legacy OMEMO1/JET PreKey envelope whose receiver `rid` is valid but whose declared peer bare JID maps to a different known identity with the same sender device id, without rewriting that other identity row or deleting its existing session. The current same-rid normal-session slice proves a wrong declared peer session fails closed without corrupting that peer's existing Signal session and without blocking later valid envelopes from the real Alice or Mallory. The 2026-05-28 OMEMO2/JET slices add the real `urn:xmpp:omemo:2` JET envelope path, select it only for v2-only recipients through `TrustManager`, wrap only the JET transport key/IV, prove helper selection plus envelope roundtrip in `Tests for omemo`, and add local FileManager/Jingle S5B receive-store plus tampered-tag cleanup coverage. The 2026-05-27 remote runner adds Linux/headless DinoX-to-DinoX OMEMO1/JET Jingle S5B send/receive proof against the canonical real server, and the 2026-05-28 `--omemo2` run adds Linux/headless DinoX-to-DinoX OMEMO2/JET Jingle S5B send/receive proof with `remote-omemo2-jingle-s5b-send-receive-ok`. The 2026-05-29 Windows MSYS2 `--omemo2` and `--omemo2 --dist-runtime` runs add Windows/headless and packaged-DLL OMEMO2/JET send/receive proof with `remote-omemo2-jingle-s5b-send-receive-ok` and `remote-omemo2-jingle-s5b-dist-runtime-send-receive-ok`. Remaining encrypted-Jingle work is a product v2-only OMEMO2/JET receiver mode runtime, packaged-client behavior beyond the recorded DinoX GUI and Libervia slices, and durable/server-ack behavior; local FileManager receive-store integration is covered by the earlier test-JET, real OMEMO1/JET S5B and real OMEMO2/JET S5B slices.

Current Jingle XEP-0260 S5B proxy activation byte-flow addendum: this slice proves the local proxy path that direct-candidate byte-flow left open. The focused `JingleFTAudit` test disables local direct candidates, injects a discovered fake XEP-0065 proxy, accepts an incoming XEP-0234/XEP-0260 offer, captures a `session-accept` with exactly one proxy candidate, injects peer `candidate-used` for that proxy CID, then verifies DinoX connects to the fake proxy using the negotiated `dstaddr`, sends an XEP-0065 `<activate/>` IQ to the proxy JID, waits for the proxy IQ result, emits Jingle `<activated cid/>`, exposes `FileTransfer.stream`, reads the declared payload and closes with `session-terminate/success`. This remains local fake-proxy activation proof only; it does not exercise the account SOCKS/Tor outbound-proxy runtime, a real relay server, remote/foreign-client interop, firewall prompts, libdino receive-store, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior, broader Windows file-transfer runtime or durable/server-ack behavior.

Current Jingle XEP-0260 S5B account-proxy remote-candidate byte-flow addendum: this slice proves the local outbound account-proxy socket path that proxy activation still left open. The focused `JingleFTAudit` test disables local direct candidates, supplies an outbound proxy URI for a fake account SOCKS5 proxy, accepts an incoming XEP-0234/XEP-0260 offer with a remote direct candidate, and captures a `session-accept` with no local direct or proxy candidates. The fake account proxy verifies the outer SOCKS5 CONNECT target is the remote candidate host/port while a DirectConnectTrap on that host/port remains untouched, then handles the inner XEP-0260 `dstaddr` SOCKS5 handshake on the same socket. The test then observes DinoX's Jingle `candidate-used`, injects peer `candidate-error`, exposes `FileTransfer.stream`, reads the declared payload and closes with `session-terminate/success`. This remains local fake account-SOCKS5 proxy proof only; it does not prove the ICE plugin/libdino account-policy wiring, a real Tor daemon, a real relay server, remote/foreign-client interop, firewall prompts, libdino receive-store, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior, broader Windows file-transfer runtime or durable/server-ack behavior.

Current Jingle XEP-0260 S5B account-proxy hostname candidate addendum: this slice sharpens the account-proxy byte-flow proof for the external/onion prerequisite without entering real Tor runtime. The focused `JingleFTAudit` test uses the same incoming XEP-0234/XEP-0260 accept path with local direct candidates disabled, but the remote candidate host is `peer-s5b.example.test`. The fake account SOCKS5 proxy must receive that exact hostname and port as the outer CONNECT target before the inner XEP-0260 `dstaddr` handshake can complete. The proof then follows the same `candidate-used`, payload read and `session-terminate/success` path. This proves hostname remote candidates are forwarded through the account SOCKS proxy instead of requiring a local direct connection; the later controlled local real Tor/onion byte-flow closes that prerequisite, while foreign-client delivery remains a separate gate.

Current ICE/libdino Jingle S5B account-policy wiring addendum: this slice proves the local libdino-to-ICE account-policy path that the account-proxy byte-flow slice left open. The ICE plugin now applies Jingle-S5B account policy through a small shared helper used by the real `stream_attached_modules` callback. The focused `Tests for ice` cases prove `Dino.build_socks5_proxy_uri(account)` reaches `JingleSocks5Bytestreams.Module.set_network_policy(...)`, normal accounts keep local direct candidates, SOCKS5 accounts use the exact configured `socks5://host:port` URI and suppress direct candidates, Tor accounts use the default `socks5://127.0.0.1:9050` URI and suppress direct candidates, and missing Jingle-S5B modules remain no-op. Additional registered-plugin tests instantiate `StreamInteractor.for_tests`, register the real ICE plugin, emit `stream_attached_modules`, and then verify the candidate-publication policy on the same stream. This remains local wiring/candidate-publication proof only; it does not prove a full real Jingle negotiation loop, a real Tor daemon, a real relay server, remote/foreign-client interop, firewall prompts, libdino receive-store, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior, broader Windows file-transfer runtime or durable/server-ack behavior.

Current real Tor S5B account-proxy no-direct addendum: this slice closes the next local Tor boundary without claiming a successful external transfer. The optional `DINOX_TEST_REAL_TOR_S5B=1` focused proof starts a private Tor process from an empty torrc and temporary data directory, applies its random loopback SOCKS listener as the Jingle-S5B outbound proxy URI, and drives the incoming remote-candidate path through the real `Jingle.Module.handle_iq_set()` and `FileTransfer.accept()` flow. DinoX publishes no local direct or proxy candidates in `session-accept`, routes the remote loopback target through Tor, Tor rejects the anonymous private-address `127.0.0.1` request, the DirectConnectTrap on that address is never hit, and no `FileTransfer.stream` is exposed. Successful Tor circuit/file byte-flow against a public/onion/external target, foreign-client delivery, firewall-prompt behavior, libdino receive-store, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior, broader Windows file-transfer runtime and durable/server-ack behavior remain separate gates.

Current real Tor onion S5B byte-flow addendum: this slice closes the controlled local onion-target boundary without claiming remote interop. The optional `DINOX_TEST_REAL_TOR_HIDDEN_SERVICE_S5B=1` proof is gated behind `-m thorough`, starts a private Tor SOCKS daemon plus temporary hidden service, forwards the onion virtual port to a controlled loopback S5B target, warms the onion service through Tor, then drives an incoming XEP-0234/XEP-0260 remote onion candidate through the production Jingle accept path. DinoX publishes no local candidates in `session-accept`, the production account-proxy path connects to the onion candidate, the controlled target validates the inner XEP-0260 `dstaddr` SOCKS5 handshake, DinoX writes `candidate-used`, peer `candidate-error` lets the selected remote candidate win, payload bytes are read through `FileTransfer.stream`, close sends `session-terminate/success`, and no Jingle session state remains. The shared test harness now removes completed async-test timeout sources, because stale 10s timeout sources from earlier async tests can otherwise mark a later long opt-in runtime proof as failed. This is controlled local onion/Tor byte-flow proof only; foreign-client delivery, packaged Windows GUI file-transfer runtime, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior, libdino account-SOCKS/Tor receive-store integration, durable jobs and server-ack behavior remain separate gates.

Jingle transport-selection fallback addendum: `ad681c9e` moves from static S5B transport-parameter proof to local Jingle transport choice using real registered XEP-0260 S5B and XEP-0261 IBB modules. The tests seed peer capabilities through a fake caps cache, prove the normal direct path still selects higher-priority S5B when both S5B and IBB are available, and prove proxy/Tor policy with no discovered S5B proxy falls back to IBB instead of selecting a direct S5B candidate or disabling Jingle globally. A focused follow-up test also covers the real `attach()` registration path for Jingle, S5B and IBB modules, so the fallback is not only proven through manual `register_transport()` calls. This is still local selection proof only; it does not open a Jingle session, transfer bytes over S5B/IBB, perform a proxy handshake, or execute foreign-client/remote interop.

Jingle XEP-0234 availability entry-point addendum: `313da161` links the already proven transport-selection boundary to the public XEP-0234 availability gate. `JingleFileTransfer.Module.is_available()` is now tested with attached ServiceDiscovery, Jingle, JingleFileTransfer, S5B and IBB modules instead of only direct parser/helper calls. The tests prove that peer disco features alone are insufficient without a locally registered streaming transport, that file-transfer and base-Jingle peer features are both required, that IBB-only support is accepted, that S5B-only support remains unavailable under proxy/Tor when no S5B proxy is discovered, and that the same proxy/Tor case is available when registered IBB can serve as fallback. This remains a local availability proof before session creation; it does not open a Jingle session, transfer bytes, prove proxy handshakes, execute remote interop, or touch OMEMO/OpenPGP/HTTP semantics.

Jingle XEP-0234 session-initiate stanza addendum: `d0fb230a` moves one step past availability into the local outbound stanza builder without entering acceptance or live transport. The tests use a bound fake stream, attached IQ/ServiceDiscovery/Jingle/JingleFileTransfer/S5B/IBB modules and a captured `write_async()` node from real `Jingle.Module.create_session()`. They prove the outgoing session-initiate IQ contains the expected fixed initiator, receiver, SID, content name/senders, XEP-0234 description, zero-byte size field, no premature XEP-0300 hash claim, and selected IBB transport stanza. The proxy/Tor fallback case confirms that a peer advertising S5B and IBB still gets an IBB session-initiate transport when S5B is unavailable because direct candidates are blocked and no S5B proxy exists. The negative case confirms missing base Jingle support fails before any IQ write. This still does not test `offer_file_stream()` acceptance waiting, live stream setup, S5B/IBB byte transfer, proxy handshakes, remote interop, HTTP upload or encrypted-body semantics.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 S5B proxy-only session-initiate addendum: this slice keeps the same captured `create_session()` boundary but exercises the privacy-sensitive proxy-only S5B path. With direct candidates disabled and a discovered S5B proxy present, the outgoing session-initiate stanza publishes S5B, not IBB, and contains only the discovered proxy candidate. The test explicitly rejects a `direct` candidate type and `127.0.0.1` host in the candidate stanza, while still proving required S5B fields such as `mode='tcp'`, SID and lowercase-hex `dstaddr`. This remains local XML/stanza proof only; real proxy connection, activation, firewall behavior, additional packaged-client Jingle-S5B interop beyond the recorded Libervia proof and remote server/runtime behavior stay open.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming session-initiate addendum: this slice moves from outbound stanza construction into the controlled receive-side proposal parser. The test builds a full incoming IQ and calls the real `Jingle.Module.handle_iq_set()` path, so `sid`/`action`, XEP-0166 content-node extraction, XEP-0234 description parsing, XEP-0261 IBB transport parsing, initial-session insertion and `JingleFileTransfer.Module.file_incoming` all run together. The positive proof stops before acceptance and byte transfer: the incoming `FileTransfer` exposes the peer, filename and size, has no accepted stream, and DinoX only acknowledges the IQ. The negative missing-size proof rejects before the file-incoming signal and before an IQ result, and the Jingle module now clears the pre-added pending session promise/session state on that `IqError`. Initial session-initiate content is now prepared as a batch before any `file_incoming` signal is emitted, so a later invalid content cannot leave a partial visible file offer. S5B transport parameters now include dispose-time listener cleanup, and direct prepared-S5B listener cleanup is now covered by a local loopback handshake/dispose proof. The duplicate-SID proof stays at the real IQ shell and proves the existing conflict response without a second incoming file signal. This does not prove content-accept, live S5B/IBB transfer, proxy activation, foreign-client interop or Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming IBB accept addendum: this slice moves one step past proposal parsing into local accept/session-accept/stream setup for IBB. The test uses the same real incoming IQ shell, accepts the returned `FileTransfer`, and proves that `FileTransfer.accept()` writes a `session-accept` stanza with the original XEP-0234 description and XEP-0261 IBB transport, sets the Jingle session `ACTIVE`, exposes a transfer input stream, and registers the underlying XEP-0047 IBB connection as `WAITING_FOR_CONNECT`. This proof found the production accept bug where DinoX called synchronous `OutputStream.close()` on an IBB stream whose output side only supports async close; `FileTransfer.accept()` now uses `close_async()` and resumes correctly. This remains local stream-setup proof only; IBB `open`/`data`/read/close flow, S5B accept, proxy activation, foreign-client interop and Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior stay open.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming IBB byte-flow addendum: this slice continues the accepted incoming IBB transfer through local XEP-0047 `open`, `data` and `close` handling. It proves the IBB connection reaches `CONNECTED`, ACKs the remote IBB stanzas, exposes decoded bytes through `FileTransferInputStream`, returns EOF after remote close, removes the connection from the IBB flag, accepts a synchronous app stream close after the remote close and sends Jingle `session-terminate` with `success`. This remains local byte-flow/app-close proof only; short-transfer size enforcement, full libdino receive-store over async-only incoming IBB streams, S5B accept/runtime/proxy behavior, foreign-client interop and Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior stay open.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming IBB short-transfer addendum: this slice closes the local short-transfer correctness gap without widening into libdino storage. A premature remote IBB `<close/>` before the declared XEP-0234 size is reached now makes `FileTransferInputStream` fail reads with `IOError.FAILED`; a subsequent app stream close sends Jingle `session-terminate` with `failed-transport`, not `success`. The IBB connection close path is made idempotent for already-disconnected streams so cleanup after remote close does not race the app close. This does not prove the higher-level libdino receive-store path, where incoming IBB is still async-only while the current store worker reads synchronously, and it does not prove remote/foreign-client behavior, foreign-client interop or Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming IBB libdino receive-store addendum: this slice closes the local libdino async-only store gap without changing the receive-store worker or writing plaintext to a pre-store temp file. The XEP-0234 `FileTransfer` exposes the active transport namespace, `JingleFileProvider` wraps only XEP-0261 IBB streams in a worker-readable adapter, and the existing worker-backed pending at-rest encryption path can keep its synchronous encryptor while the wrapper dispatches reads back to the original async context. The focused libdino proof uses synthetic async-only streams, verifies that async reads are used, decrypts the committed local file back to the original payload, checks `COMPLETE` plus pending-file cleanup, and covers cancellation while the store worker is waiting on an adapter-backed async read. The adapter now also fails with `WOULD_BLOCK` instead of hanging indefinitely if its operation cannot be dispatched to the captured async context. This remains local adapter/store proof; live XMPP/Jingle runtime was covered later for IBB and direct S5B, while foreign-client interop, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior and Windows runtime stay open.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming IBB FileManager provider-runtime addendum: this slice connects the previously separate local proofs through the production libdino provider path. A fake connected stream supplies the real XMPP modules, the incoming IQ flows through `Jingle.Module.handle_iq_set()`, `JingleFileProvider.file_incoming`, `FileManager.handle_incoming_file`, trusted-roster auto-download, `JingleFileProvider.download()`, real XEP-0234 accept, XEP-0047 open/data/close, `WorkerReadableInputStream`, receive-store at-rest encryption, verified commit and final `COMPLETE`. The proof intentionally sends remote `<close/>` immediately after the final `<data/>`; this exposed and fixes the XEP-0047 close/drain race by keeping already received chunks readable after remote close until the queue is drained. This is still local fake-stream provider/runtime proof for IBB only; direct S5B provider/runtime is covered by the follow-up below, while real server networking, foreign-client interop, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior, broader Windows file-transfer runtime and durable/server-ack behavior remain separate gates.

Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming S5B FileManager provider-runtime addendum: this slice closes the direct-S5B libdino receive-store gap without changing production code. Current Jingle XEP-0234 incoming S5B real Tor onion FileManager provider-runtime addendum: this slice connects the controlled real Tor/onion S5B byte-flow to the production libdino receive-store path. Current remote scripted-raw Jingle S5B provider-runtime addendum: this slice extends the real-server Jingle S5B receive-store runtime evidence beyond the DinoX-pair `offer_file_stream()` sender path. The scripted sender uses a normal logged-in XMPP stream only for transport, then unregisters the sender-side DinoX Jingle handlers, builds the XEP-0234/XEP-0260 `session-initiate` manually, publishes a loopback direct S5B candidate, waits for DinoX `candidate-used`, sends `candidate-error`, writes the generated payload over the selected S5B socket and waits for `session-terminate/success`. Current remote standalone foreign-sender Jingle S5B provider-runtime addendum: this slice runs the existing foreign-client receiver harness against a small independent Python stdlib sender. Current remote standalone proxy no-direct Jingle S5B addendum: this slice turns the independent stdlib sender into a negative privacy-routing smoke against a real server. The receiver account connects through a local per-account SOCKS5 guard proxy; the sender offers only a direct loopback XEP-0260 S5B candidate; the receiver applies the same Jingle S5B network-policy surface used by production account proxy/Tor wiring; the sender verifies the receiver `session-accept` published no direct candidates and then waits for clean failure without any connection to its direct S5B listener; the guard proxy must record a rejected local S5B CONNECT matching the exact offered direct candidate host/port; and FileManager must fail the incoming transfer without a verified storage path, plaintext release, persisted `COMPLETE`, or pending receive files. The supporting ICE test now proves the production ICE plugin applies the same account policy to the incoming parse/session-accept path, while the remote smoke directly applies that policy surface because the minimal libdino remote harness does not load the ICE plugin. This is a real-server per-account SOCKS no-direct proof against the independent stdlib sender, not a packaged third-party client proof, not the proxy-success receive-store proof, not Tor exit-to-public-candidate success/onion remote-candidate byte-flow, not Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior and not durable/server-ack behavior; the corresponding Windows-MSYS2 companion pass is tracked separately.

Current remote standalone proxy-success Jingle S5B addendum: this slice flips the guard proxy from negative privacy-routing to a controlled successful byte-flow through the receiver's account SOCKS path. The receiver account still connects through the local SOCKS5 guard proxy; the independent stdlib sender still offers only a direct loopback XEP-0260 S5B candidate; the sender verifies the receiver `session-accept` published no direct candidates; the guard is allowed to forward only the exact offered candidate host/port; and the normal receiver harness must complete receive-store with persisted `COMPLETE`, verified stored payload, no pending receive files and no leftover Jingle session state. This is real-server per-account SOCKS successful S5B byte-flow proof against the independent stdlib sender, not a packaged third-party client proof, not Tor exit-to-public-candidate success/onion remote-candidate byte-flow, not Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior and not durable/server-ack behavior; the corresponding Windows-MSYS2 companion pass is tracked separately.

Current remote real-Tor no-direct Jingle S5B addendum: this slice reruns the independent stdlib sender negative privacy-routing proof with the receiver account configured for an isolated local Tor SOCKS daemon instead of the local guard proxy. The receiver is configured with `proxy_type=tor`, applies the no-direct Jingle S5B policy, publishes no direct candidates in `session-accept`, attempts the sender's direct loopback candidate through the Tor account proxy, and fails the incoming transfer without a verified storage path, plaintext release, persisted `COMPLETE` or pending receive files. The sender verifies that its direct listener was not contacted and reports the no-direct marker. On 2026-05-25 the boundary first passed manually and then passed through `scripts/run-remote-jingle-s5b-smoke.sh --standalone-sender-real-tor-no-direct` with redacted DinoX test accounts. This closes the real-server private-candidate no-direct boundary for the receiver account with the receiver configured for real Tor SOCKS; it is not packaged third-party client proof, not Tor exit-to-public-candidate success, not onion remote-candidate byte-flow, not Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior and not durable/server-ack behavior; the corresponding Windows-MSYS2 companion pass is tracked separately.

Current remote real-Tor onion-success Jingle S5B addendum: this slice turns the existing controlled local onion proof into a real-server standalone-sender receive-store smoke. The runner starts an isolated local Tor SOCKS daemon with a temporary hidden service mapped to the stdlib sender's local S5B listener, warms the onion service through Tor, configures the receiver account with `proxy_type=tor`, and runs the normal `/RemoteJingleS5bSmoke/incoming_foreign_client_s5b_env` success path. The sender advertises the `.onion` host and virtual port as its remote S5B candidate, the receiver publishes no direct candidates in `session-accept`, DinoX selects the onion candidate through the Tor account proxy, payload bytes traverse the onion-routed S5B socket, and FileManager completes receive-store with persisted `COMPLETE`, verified stored payload, no pending files and no leftover Jingle session state. On 2026-05-25 this passed through `scripts/run-remote-jingle-s5b-smoke.sh --standalone-sender-real-tor-onion-success` with redacted DinoX test accounts; on 2026-05-27 the same mode passed on the Windows MSYS2 VM with `/mingw64/bin/tor` after sender-runner timing fixes for hidden-service warmup, XMPP polling and early closed post-warmup hidden-service target connections. This closes real-server onion remote-candidate byte-flow for the independent stdlib sender with a controlled temporary onion target, plus the corresponding Windows-MSYS2 build-tree/headless receive-store boundary; it is not packaged third-party client proof, not Tor exit-to-public-candidate success, not packaged Windows GUI proof, not Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior and not durable/server-ack behavior.

Current remote real-Tor public-candidate Jingle S5B note: `scripts/run-remote-jingle-s5b-smoke.sh --standalone-sender-real-tor-public-success` is now an executed Tor exit-to-public-candidate proof. It requires `DINOX_REMOTE_JINGLE_S5B_PUBLIC_CANDIDATE_HOST` and `DINOX_REMOTE_JINGLE_S5B_PUBLIC_CANDIDATE_PORT`, rejects obvious local/private/onion hosts during preflight, starts the receiver with an isolated local Tor SOCKS daemon, monitors Tor's local ControlPort for a `SENTCONNECT` or `SUCCEEDED` STREAM event to the exact advertised public host/port, and lets the independent stdlib sender advertise that public S5B candidate while binding a configurable local listener. Optional `DINOX_REMOTE_JINGLE_S5B_PUBLIC_LISTEN_HOST` and `DINOX_REMOTE_JINGLE_S5B_PUBLIC_LISTEN_PORT` cover NAT or tunnel mappings. On 2026-05-26 this passed against `dinox.im` with redacted DinoX test accounts and a generated Pinggy raw TCP public candidate (`*.run.pinggy-free.link`) mapped to the sender's local listener. On 2026-05-27 the same mode passed on the Windows MSYS2 VM with `/mingw64/bin/tor`, advertising public candidate `45.84.199.125:47777` through a temporary self-controlled DinoX-server TCP forward to the Windows listener and completing with `WINDOWS_TOR_PUBLIC_HARNESS_EXIT 0` at `2026-05-27T00:35:51+02:00`. The run also fixed the runner-side Tor proof monitor: the Tor control port now exports out of the helper without Bash dynamic-scope shadowing, the monitor reads STREAM events with `sock.recv()` instead of a timeout-poisoned file object, and it parses the STREAM target from the correct Tor field. This closes Tor exit-to-public-candidate success for a temporary public raw TCP candidate plus the corresponding Windows-MSYS2 build-tree/headless boundary. The later strict `--standalone-sender-real-tor-stable-public-success` permanent-endpoint gate passed on 2026-06-01 against the self-owned raw-direct endpoint and printed `remote-jingle-s5b-stable-public-endpoint-ok`; the packaged Windows GUI raw-direct receiver/open/export proof for the same endpoint is recorded below. The separate stable public XEP-0065 proxy gate is also recorded below.

Current remote packaged Libervia Jingle S5B note: on 2026-05-26 a locally extracted Ubuntu Noble `libervia-cli`/`libervia-backend` 0.9.0 package sent a real XEP-0234/XEP-0260 S5B file offer to the DinoX receiver harness on `dinox.im`. The run used an isolated temporary Libervia backend/profile, did not install packages system-wide, did not print secrets, targeted the receiver's bound full JID, and passed the existing `/RemoteJingleS5bSmoke/incoming_foreign_client_s5b_env` receive-store assertions. Two Libervia-specific headless boundaries are recorded with the proof: the default extracted package hit a MiniUPnPc/NAT-PORT `Exception('Success')` before `session-initiate`, and disabling NAT-PORT still required `General/allow_get_ip=false` to avoid a headless external-IP confirmation. With NAT-PORT unavailable and `allow_get_ip=false`, Libervia emitted `session-initiate`, S5B candidates, `candidate-used` and checksum/session-info, while DinoX completed receive-store and Jingle cleanup. This closes one packaged Libervia headless-safe S5B interop slice; it does not claim default Libervia NAT-PORT behavior, Gajim GUI automation, Tor exit-to-public-candidate success, packaged Windows GUI file-transfer runtime, Windows/packaged encrypted Jingle payload behavior or durable/server-ack behavior.

Current packaged third-party Gajim feasibility note: the locally installed Gajim Flatpak was checked on 2026-05-25 as `org.gajim.Gajim` 2.4.6. `gajim-remote` exposes roster, account, status and text-message commands only; it has no stable send-file/Jingle file-transfer CLI or D-Bus method. The visible package file-send path is HTTP upload, while Jingle file-transfer internals are not exposed as a supported non-GUI sender surface. The packaged-client category now has Libervia coverage above, but Gajim 2.4.6 Flatpak remains a separate unproven GUI/manual-client path until a supported send-file surface appears or a separate manual GUI run is recorded with concrete stanza/runtime evidence.

Current remote foreign terminal HTTP upload note: `scripts/run-remote-foreign-http-upload-smoke.sh` uses `go-sendxmpp --http-upload` as a non-DinoX terminal sender for a one-to-one HTTP upload. On 2026-05-26 the runner passed against `dinox.im` with redacted disposable accounts and a temporarily extracted `go-sendxmpp` 0.8.3 binary. The sender uploads via XEP-0363 and sends an OOB/body URL; DinoX receives through `FileProvider` plus `FileManager`, stores the payload at rest, decrypts it for verification, checks size/SHA-256 and leaves no pending receive files. This closes a real-server terminal-client HTTP-upload receive boundary only; it must not be counted as packaged third-party Jingle S5B or Tor-public proof. Encrypted payloads, private-MUC fanout, Windows runtime, upload retention/deletion and durable/server-ack behavior remain separate gates.

It validates the required environment, compiles `libdino/libdino-test` with remote MUC env unset, runs `/RemotePrivateMucSmoke/private_muc_bootstrap_affiliation_snapshot_env`, and prints a short success marker while avoiding raw JID/member-list output on successful runs. On 2026-05-25 this runner passed against `dinox.im`/`conference.dinox.im` with redacted disposable DinoX test accounts. Current remote private-MUC HTTP file-send runner note: `scripts/run-remote-private-muc-http-smoke.sh` compiles `plugins/http-files/http-files-test` with remote env unset, then opt-in runs `/RemotePrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_unencrypted_http_send_receive_env` with two disposable DinoX accounts and an optional third DinoX receiver. The smoke creates a generated members-only, non-anonymous room, joins sender and receiver accounts, proves the complete affiliation snapshot contains every configured account, sends a tiny unencrypted file through public `FileManager.send_file()`, requests a real XEP-0363 slot, performs a real HTTPS `PUT`, receives the SFS/HTTP transfer on each configured receiver account, downloads it and verifies the stored payload hash. On 2026-05-25 it passed against `dinox.im`/`conference.dinox.im` with redacted disposable DinoX test accounts, including the optional third-account fanout mode. This also exposed and fixed an unencrypted MUC HTTP send hang where `HttpFileSender` waited for a reflected `server_id` even though the SFS source attachment references the already available origin/stanza id. This is unencrypted DinoX-to-DinoX private-MUC HTTP/SFS runtime proof only; it is not OMEMO/OpenPGP private-MUC send proof, not packaged third-party behavior, not public-MUC behavior, not Windows runtime, not server-side upload retention/deletion proof and not durable/server-ack/MAM-retention proof.

Current remote OpenPGP private-MUC HTTP file-send/receive runner note: `scripts/run-remote-openpgp-private-muc-http-smoke.sh` compiles `plugins/openpgp/openpgp-test` with remote OpenPGP MUC env unset, then opt-in runs `/RemoteOpenPgpPrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_xep0374_http_send_receive_env` by default, `/RemoteOpenPgpPrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_xep0374_pubsub_http_send_receive_env` with `--pubsub-discovery`, or `/RemoteOpenPgpPrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_xep0027_http_send_receive_env` with `--legacy-xep0027`, using two disposable DinoX accounts. The smoke creates a generated members-only, non-anonymous room, joins sender and receiver accounts, proves live private-room features plus a complete member/admin/owner affiliation snapshot, keeps the receiver online, sends a tiny file through public `FileManager.send_file()` with `Encryption.PGP`, requests a real XEP-0363 slot, performs a real HTTPS `PUT`, receives the encrypted body-url fanout on the second DinoX account, maps `room/nick` to the sender's real bare JID before trust/signature checks, downloads/decrypts/stores the file through the normal HTTP provider plus `PgpFileDecryptor` path, and verifies the stored plaintext hash. The XEP-0374 paths create isolated temporary OpenPGP XMPP keys and either seed fresh local XEP-0373 state or publish/fetch keys through real remote XEP-0373 PubSub. The legacy XEP-0027 path creates isolated temporary email-UID keys, stores account legacy keys plus keyserver-style legacy contact keys, waits for signed-presence activity to mark both directions announced, and verifies no XEP-0373/XEP-0374 contact-key state is populated. The proof checks sender-side phase order through `STORING -> ENCRYPTING -> REQUESTING_SLOT -> UPLOADING -> ANNOUNCING -> COMPLETE`, no outer OOB/SFS/source/attachment URL leakage, fallback body without the HTTP URL, sender plus receiver recipient packets, valid sender signature, preserved filename, uploaded `.pgp` plaintext matching the original payload, receiver `GROUPCHAT` transfer state, plaintext release only after `COMPLETE`, no pending receive files and matching receiver-side payload size/SHA-256 after at-rest decrypt. XEP-0374 additionally verifies signcrypt recipient/body containment; XEP-0027 verifies the legacy `jabber:x:encrypted` body-url payload, legacy fallback body and absence of XEP-0374 payload markup. On 2026-05-25 the seeded XEP-0374 path passed against `dinox.im`/`conference.dinox.im` with two redacted disposable DinoX test accounts. On 2026-05-26 `--pubsub-discovery` and `--legacy-xep0027` passed against the same server; the legacy path also passed from the Windows MSYS2 build-tree checkout. This is DinoX-to-DinoX private-MUC OpenPGP HTTP send/receive runtime proof for locally seeded XEP-0373/XEP-0374, remote PubSub-discovered XEP-0373/XEP-0374 and legacy XEP-0027 signed-presence key activity. It is not external OpenPGP HKP/VKS keyserver proof, not Gajim or packaged third-party behavior, not public-MUC behavior, not packaged Windows GUI proof, not server-side upload retention/deletion proof and not durable/server-ack/MAM-retention proof.

Current remote OMEMO private-MUC HTTP file-send/receive runner note: `scripts/run-remote-omemo-private-muc-http-smoke.sh` compiles `plugins/omemo/omemo-test` with remote OMEMO MUC env unset, then opt-in runs `/RemoteOmemoPrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_omemo1_http_send_receive_env` by default, `/RemoteOmemoPrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_omemo1_pubsub_http_send_receive_env` with `--pubsub-discovery`, `/RemoteOmemoPrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_omemo2_http_send_receive_env` with `--omemo2`, or `/RemoteOmemoPrivateMucHttpSmoke/private_muc_omemo2_pubsub_http_send_receive_env` with `--omemo2-pubsub-discovery`, using two disposable DinoX accounts and an optional third DinoX receiver. The smoke creates a generated members-only, non-anonymous room, joins sender and receiver accounts, proves live private-room features plus a complete member/admin/owner affiliation snapshot, installs isolated OMEMO runtime state, then sends a tiny file through public `FileManager.send_file()` with `Encryption.OMEMO`, requests a real XEP-0363 slot, performs a real HTTPS `PUT`, captures the outgoing body-only OMEMO body-url stanza, receives the transfer on every configured DinoX receiver account, downloads it through the normal HTTP provider path and verifies the decrypted stored payload hash. The default OMEMO1 and `--omemo2` paths seed sender-to-receiver pre-key sessions in the temporary test stores; `--pubsub-discovery` deliberately skips those seeds and instead waits for real remote legacy OMEMO1 PubSub device-list/bundle discovery, identity-key rows and libomemo sessions for every configured receiver plus the receiver-side sender lookup needed for private-MUC decrypt. `--omemo2-pubsub-discovery` deliberately skips seeded OMEMO2 sessions and trusted-device rows, temporarily publishes controlled v2-only OMEMO PEP device-list and bundle state for disposable accounts, requires `DINOX_REMOTE_OMEMO_MUC_ALLOW_PEP_MUTATION=1`, waits for real remote v2 PubSub discovery plus libomemo sessions in both directions, and restores and verifies normal v1+v2 current-device PEP state during cleanup. On 2026-05-25 OMEMO1 passed against `dinox.im`/`conference.dinox.im` with redacted disposable DinoX test accounts; the optional third-account fanout mode also passed on that date. On 2026-05-25 OMEMO2/SCE also passed against the same server with two redacted disposable accounts and again with optional third-account fanout. On 2026-05-25 `--pubsub-discovery` passed against the same server with optional third-receiver fanout. On 2026-05-26 `--omemo2-pubsub-discovery` passed against the same server with two redacted disposable accounts and again with optional third-account fanout. The proof checks sender-side phase order through `STORING -> REQUESTING_SLOT -> UPLOADING -> ANNOUNCING -> COMPLETE`, no outer OOB/SFS/source/attachment URL leakage, fallback body without the `aesgcm://` secret or HTTP URL, protocol-specific OMEMO recipient keys for every configured receiver device, OMEMO2 no-legacy-fallback selection when OMEMO2 is used, matching receiver-side `GROUPCHAT` conversations, complete receive-store state, plaintext release only after completion, no pending receive files and matching payload size/SHA-256 after decrypt. This is DinoX-to-DinoX private-MUC HTTP send/receive runtime proof for OMEMO1, remote PubSub-discovered OMEMO1, seeded OMEMO2/SCE and controlled remote PubSub-discovered OMEMO2/SCE with optional third-receiver fanout. It is not Gajim or packaged third-party behavior, not public-MUC behavior, not the Windows-MSYS2 seeded and PubSub-discovered OMEMO1/OMEMO2 passes are recorded below, and it is not server-side upload retention/deletion proof or durable/server-ack/MAM-retention proof.

The run covered static documentation, translation, hidden-Unicode, Python, shell, release-helper, package metadata and Flatpak manifest checks; strict build with zero compiler warnings; Meson suites for main, xmpp-vala, libdino, omemo, openpgp, bot-features, http-files and mqtt; plus DB CLI and DB integration tests. Current Windows packaged OpenPGP shutdown note: on 2026-05-26 the Windows VM post-unlock tray-Quit/process-list smoke passed with the packaged `dist\dinox.exe` from `C:\msys64\home\info\dinox`. The run used an interactive scheduled task, unlocked the existing encrypted DinoX profile with a supplied DB password without logging the plaintext, reached the tray-created post-unlock marker, sent Ctrl+Q, observed tray cleanup, `MQTT plugin: shutdown`, `OpenPGP: Stopped app-scoped GnuPG components (exit=0)`, no force-exit fallback marker, and no remaining `dinox.exe`, `gpg.exe`, `gpgconf.exe` or `gpg-agent.exe` processes after quit. The failing pre-fix run proved the root cause: packaged `gpgconf.exe --kill all` needed `dist\bin\gpg-connect-agent.exe`; `scripts/update_dist.sh` now copies that helper and includes it in the helper dependency check. A later packaged WM_ENDSESSION smoke on the same date passed through the hidden Win32 tray window: `WM_QUERYENDSESSION` allowed shutdown, `WM_ENDSESSION` ran the new synchronous system-shutdown path, OpenPGP drained the GPG helper worker and secret-key async worker before `gpgconf --kill all`, systray cleanup closed the native log, no force-exit fallback marker appeared, and no DinoX/GnuPG helper process remained. The destructive true OS logoff proof then passed at commit `28b841e8`: packaged DinoX reached the post-unlock tray state, the interactive driver triggered `shutdown.exe /l`, the out-of-session watcher validated the same runtime and systray markers under Windows session-manager timing, and no DinoX/GnuPG helper process remained after logoff. This closes the packaged post-unlock quit/GPG cleanup gate, the packaged WM_ENDSESSION code-path/process-list gate and the packaged true OS logoff process-list gate only; it is not the broader Windows file-transfer runtime matrix, not encrypted receiver/open/export proof, and not durable job/resume proof.
